 ### Changed
 
-- **Witness format** (`.opencode/wolf-sync-state.json`): a bare hash string becomes `{ hash, origin: 'render' | 'keep', base: 'X.Y.Z' }`. `origin: 'keep'` records accepted local content, so the next pass does not silently revert it; a file accepted at an older base only gets an info line (`locally accepted at <base>, base <X.Y.Z>`). Legacy string records are normalized on read (`origin: 'render'`, `base` from the file stamp) — no migration step, the new shape is written on the next saving operation.
+- **Witness format** (`.opencode/wolf-sync-state.json`): a bare hash string becomes `{ hash, origin: 'render' | 'keep', base: 'X.Y.Z' }`. `origin: 'keep'` records accepted local content, so the next pass does not silently revert it; a file accepted at an older base only gets an info line (`locally accepted (keep) at <base>, base <X.Y.Z>`). Legacy string records are normalized on read (`origin: 'render'`, `base` from the file stamp) — no migration step, the new shape is written on the next saving operation.
 - **`--check` is strictly read-only** in both modes and always exits `0`. `--here --check` is dispatched before the project pass, so it can no longer fall through into a writing run. A missing or unreliable update map degrades the summary to stamps only and says so.
 - **`deprecated` targets are report-only**: they are no longer a decision (and get no decision number) — the file stays in the release, is not updated, and is shown as a note. A deprecated target deleted by hand and re-created still reports `conflict`; it is resolved by path. No deprecated targets exist in the 2.20.0 map.
 - **Inspection vocabulary**: `identical` in `--check` now means "nothing to decide on this pass", not "byte-equal" (a healed stamp drift is `identical` too, and does not show as `behind`).

 - **`protect` frontmatter key (REQ-05)**: `protect: true` (hold the local content forever) or `protect: X.Y.Z` (hold and pin the base version) in the YAML frontmatter of any wolf-managed agent/skill/command. The key is inserted as the last line of the frontmatter block (line-wise edit — no YAML re-dump, comments and key order survive) and validated structurally before the file is written. Empty, `~`, `null` or any other value is treated as **held + warning** (fail-safe, never "silently unprotected"); `protect: false` equals no key. Normalization — the file content with every `protect:` line removed — is the single comparison base for both the base set and the witness, so adding, changing or removing the key never produces a false `mutated`. Duplicate keys: the first one wins, the rest is a warning (`protect declared twice, first value wins`). A protected file is never overwritten (not by a pass, not by `--yes`, not by stamp healing) and never deleted: a `removed` target that is protected stays on disk with code `protected` and a hint. A stamped orphan file with `protect` is a sanctioned local variation — not a conflict, not a warning.
 - **`protect` never reaches the model (spike R-01, design §5)**: the key is read by wolf itself and stays in the file. Verified live on opencode `v2.1.4` against a real provider: the request returns `200`, and `protect` does not appear in the model request body at all — for a primary agent and for a subagent called through `task` (capture-proxy evidence in the wave design). Residual risk: a future opencode release could start forwarding unknown agent keys; the structural frontmatter check before every write stays for that reason, and one live run is kept as a regression guard.
 - **Protections section in `wolf upgrade --check` (REQ-09)**: printed always — park and `--here`, empty park included (`Protections: none`); lists each protected file with its value and `behind (base X.Y.Z)` / `ok` / `drift: n/a`, then a `release:` hint. `kept` files (accepted local content) are reported separately as `kept (local)` and do not make the project look behind. Value outside the grammar is shown as `(unrecognized — treated as protected)`.

 ## Limitations, stated plainly
 
 - **No auto-merge of content.** Only whole-file `take`/`keep`. If you need a merged file, take the base and re-apply your edit, or protect the file and keep maintaining it.
-- **`diverged` faces are not addressable.** They are printed in their own section (`faces (steward zone): … diverged (vX.Y.Z)`) and keep exit `2`, but there is no `--resolve` verb for them: a face is memory, and memory is mutated through the complaint loop and the Steward.
+- **`diverged` faces are not addressable.** They are printed in their own section — `steward zone (not addressable by --resolve): <N>`, then one `<project> <file> diverged v<version>` line each — and keep exit `2`, but there is no `--resolve` verb for them: a face is memory, and memory is mutated through the complaint loop and the Steward.
 - **`orphan` files have no base.** A stamped file whose target disappeared from the package can only be held (`=protect`) or deleted by hand; `take` is refused.
 - **Backups are not garbage-collected** (above), and they do not preserve file metadata.
 - **Exit model is unchanged** from 2.19.0: `0` done, `1` failed, `2` done with attention (`diverged` included).

 - **`protect` frontmatter key (REQ-05)**: `protect: true` (hold the local content forever) or `protect: X.Y.Z` (hold and pin the base version) in the YAML frontmatter of any wolf-managed agent/skill/command. The key is inserted as the last line of the frontmatter block (line-wise edit — no YAML re-dump, comments and key order survive) and validated structurally before the file is written. Empty, `~`, `null` or any other value is treated as **held + warning** (fail-safe, never "silently unprotected"); `protect: false` equals no key. Normalization — the file content with every `protect:` line removed — is the single comparison base for both the base set and the witness, so adding, changing or removing the key never produces a false `mutated`. Duplicate keys: the first one wins, the rest is a warning (`protect declared twice, first value wins`). A protected file is never overwritten (not by a pass, not by `--yes`, not by stamp healing) and never deleted: a `removed` target that is protected stays on disk with code `protected` and a hint. A stamped orphan file with `protect` is a sanctioned local variation — not a conflict, not a warning.
 - **`protect` never reaches the model (spike R-01, design §5)**: the key is read by wolf itself and stays in the file. Verified live on opencode `v2.1.4` against a real provider: the request returns `200`, and `protect` does not appear in the model request body at all — for a primary agent and for a subagent called through `task` (capture-proxy evidence in the wave design). Residual risk: a future opencode release could start forwarding unknown agent keys; the structural frontmatter check before every write stays for that reason, and one live run is kept as a regression guard.

 - **`--check` is strictly read-only** in both modes and always exits `0`. `--here --check` is dispatched before the project pass, so it can no longer fall through into a writing run. A missing or unreliable update map degrades the summary to stamps only and says so.
 - **`deprecated` targets are report-only**: they are no longer a decision (and get no decision number) — the file stays in the release, is not updated, and is shown as a note.  - **Inspection vocabulary**: `identical` in `--check` now means "nothing to decide on this pass", not "byte-equal" (a healed stamp drift is `identical` too, and does not show as `behind`).

# Changelog

All notable changes to this project are documented in this file.

Format: [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.1.0/); versioning: [SemVer](https://semver.org/).

## [2.20.0] - 2026-10-25

### Added — Upgrade UX: owner intentions, named decisions, silent pass

- **File names in the pass and in `--check` (REQ-01)**: every needs-decision outcome (`mutated` / `diverged` / `conflict`) and every `differ` is now printed with the file path relative to the project root, not just as a counter — e.g. `mutated: .opencode/agents/worker-reviewer.md · 1 protected (intentional)`. Counters are preserved; names are added next to them. Long lists are wrapped by the terminal renderer, not truncated.
- **`wolf upgrade --resolve` (REQ-06)**: owner decisions without a pass — `--resolve <file|N>=take|keep|protect|pin`, repeatable, comma-separated lists allowed. Targets: `<rel-path>` (unique in the park or in `--here`), `<project>/<rel-path>`, or the decision number `N` from `--review`/`--check` (a path that starts with a digit is written as `./3=…`). All parse and scope errors are collected and printed before anything is written; decisions are applied in registry order, each one idempotent (`backup → file → witness`). A protected file, an out-of-map `orphan`, and a non-mutated target are all addressable by path; `take` is the only verb stronger than `protect` (it drops the key, takes the base and saves the local content to a backup). `keep` on a `conflict` is refused with a hint (`use protect (hold) or take (base)`), `protect` on a file without YAML frontmatter is refused (`cannot protect <path>: no YAML frontmatter — add one or use keep`). `--resolve` never runs the npm phase or the pass.
- **`wolf upgrade --review` (REQ-07)**: walks the decisions one by one with a real diff (`t` take / `k` keep / `p` protect / `v` pin / `s` skip, plus `a` — apply to all similar targets of the same kind+code, `q` — quit; accepted decisions stay applied). Without a TTY it writes nothing and prints the numbered recipe plus the exact commands, exit `2`. The same review engine drives `--resolve`.
- **`wolf upgrade --show` (REQ-08)**: read-only view of one target — current `protect` value, base, file stamp, witness, drift and the `local → base` diff (what `take` would give you), plus the ready-to-run `release:` command. Works for protected files and for files outside the update map (there: `base: n/a (file outside the update map)`, no diff, no `take` hint).
- **`protect` frontmatter key (REQ-05)**: `protect: true` (hold the local content forever) or `protect: X.Y.Z` (hold and pin the base version) in the YAML frontmatter of any wolf-managed agent/skill/command. The key is inserted as the last line of the frontmatter block (line-wise edit — no YAML re-dump, comments and key order survive) and validated structurally before the file is written. Empty, `~`, `null` or any other value is treated as **held + warning** (fail-safe, never "silently unprotected"); `protect: false` equals no key. Normalization — the file content with every `protect:` line removed — is the single comparison base for both the base set and the witness, so adding, changing or removing the key never produces a false `mutated`. Duplicate keys: the first one wins, the rest is a warning (`protect declared twice, first value wins`). A protected file is never overwritten (not by a pass, not by `--yes`, not by stamp healing) and never deleted: a `removed` target that is protected stays on disk with code `protected` and a hint. A stamped orphan file with `protect` is a sanctioned local variation — not a conflict, not a warning.
- **`protect` never reaches the model (spike R-01, design §5)**: the key is read by wolf itself and stays in the file. Verified live on opencode `v2.1.4` against a real provider: the request returns `200`, and `protect` does not appear in the model request body at all — for a primary agent and for a subagent called through `task` (capture-proxy evidence in the wave design). Residual risk: a future opencode release could start forwarding unknown agent keys; the structural frontmatter check before every write stays for that reason, and one live run is kept as a regression guard.
- **Protections section in `wolf upgrade --check` (REQ-09)**: printed always — park and `--here`, empty park included (`Protections: none`); lists each protected file with its value and `behind (base X.Y.Z)` / `ok` / `drift: n/a`, then a `release:` hint. `kept` files (accepted local content) are reported separately as `kept (local)` and do not make the project look behind. Value outside the grammar is shown as `(unrecognized — treated as protected)`.
- **Backups before every overwrite (REQ-10)**: `--yes` and `take` copy the local file to `.wolf/backup/upgrade/<YYYY-MM-DD>/<rel-path>` (on a second overwrite of the same file on the same day the copy becomes `<name>-2.<ext>`, then `-3`, … — the first free name, nothing is ever overwritten inside the backup tree), then write the base, then the witness. Deleting a `removed` target copies it first for the same reason. The digest names both the file and the copy: `took base for .opencode/agents/foo.md (local saved: .wolf/backup/upgrade/2026-10-25/.opencode/agents/foo.md)`. Restoring is a plain copy back; the file then is a normal `mutated` and can be frozen legally with `--resolve <file>=protect`. Backup tree growth has no garbage collector (see Upgrade notes).
- **`--json` (REQ-11)**: one machine-readable document on stdout, nothing else — `wolf.upgrade/1` for modes `pass|here|resolve|show` (fields: `schema`, `exit`, `mode`, `cli`, `plan`, `projects[]` with `files[]`/`protected[]`/`backups[]`, `decisions[]`, `diverged[]`, `protections[]`) and `wolf.upgrade.check/1` for `--check`. `decisions[].n` is the same numbering as the text renderer. Stability: additive fields in minors, renames/removals only with a new major (`/2`).
- **`wolf init` repairs `mcp.wolf` (REQ-04)**: a minimal `init` on a project whose platform config already exists restores a missing or corrupted `mcp.wolf` entry (alongside `default_agent` / `subagent_depth`) and keeps the rest of the JSON — the `run wolf init` advice from `wolf doctor` is now actionable. The config is never created if it was absent (minimal init stays minimal), foreign MCP entries are untouched, `--platform` keeps its replace semantics, and a second run reports `unchanged` (idempotent).
- **Onboarding block v3 (REQ-03)**: the `AGENTS.md` block gains the owner-intention line (`protect` / `wolf upgrade --review` / `--resolve`) and ships as `<!-- wolf:onboarding v3 -->`. Block detection is version-agnostic, so a `v2` block is recognized and updated in place instead of appending a second one; only the text between the markers is rewritten — everything above and below stays byte-for-byte (a CRLF file keeps its EOL). User text _inside_ the block is not preserved: the block is wolf's zone. Broken markers (`open` without `close`) are treated as foreign under the `wx` policy — reported as `onboarding markers broken` in the pass details and in `--check`, never turned into a decision. `init` on a stamped 2.19.0 file also aligns the stamp in place; `upgrade` does not append a block to an unstamped `AGENTS.md` without markers (only `init` does).
- **`diverged` faces are visible and honest**: memory faces whose content drifted from the template are printed as their own section — `steward zone (not addressable by --resolve): <N>`, then one `<project> <file> diverged v<version>` line each — keep exit `2` while they exist, and are **not** addressable — a face is memory, so the mutation goes through the complaint loop and the Steward, not through `--resolve`.

### Changed

- **Witness format** (`.opencode/wolf-sync-state.json`): a bare hash string becomes `{ hash, origin: 'render' | 'keep', base: 'X.Y.Z' }`. `origin: 'keep'` records accepted local content, so the next pass does not silently revert it; a file accepted at an older base only gets an info line (`locally accepted (keep) at <base>, base <X.Y.Z>`). Legacy string records are normalized on read (`origin: 'render'`, `base` from the file stamp) — no migration step, the new shape is written on the next saving operation.
- **`--check` is strictly read-only** in both modes and always exits `0`. `--here --check` is dispatched before the project pass, so it can no longer fall through into a writing run. A missing or unreliable update map degrades the summary to stamps only and says so.
- **`deprecated` targets are report-only**: they are no longer a decision (and get no decision number) — the file stays in the release, is not updated, and is shown as a note. A deprecated target deleted by hand and re-created still reports `conflict`; it is resolved by path. No deprecated targets exist in the 2.20.0 map.
- **Inspection vocabulary**: `identical` in `--check` now means "nothing to decide on this pass", not "byte-equal" (a healed stamp drift is `identical` too, and does not show as `behind`).
- **`kept` files**: a locally accepted file is not a decision and does not make a project `behind`; it is listed as `kept (local)` with the base it was accepted at.
- **`wolf upgrade --check --json`** gains `protections[]` and `diverged[]`, and each project gains `differ[]` / `missing[]` / `mutated[]` / `foreign[]` / `details[]` / `protected[]` — no more counter-only reporting for machines.

### Fixed

- **False `mutated` from stamp drift (REQ-02)**: a stamped file whose content is byte-identical to the fresh base except for the `wolf:rendered …` line is silently re-rendered, the stamp is updated and the witness refreshed — outcome `updated` with reason `stamp drift healed (base <v>)`, not a needs-decision, and not `behind` in `--check`. The stamp strip regex lives in one place (`stamp.ts`), so healing and stamp insertion cannot diverge.
- **The eternal `behind · 1 differ` on `AGENTS.md` (REQ-03)**: the onboarding block is no longer compared as "the whole file differs"; a `v2` block on a 2.19.0 project becomes `v3` in place, the pass is idempotent, and `--check` shows `current` afterwards.
- **`wolf doctor` advice that could not be followed (REQ-04)**: `init` now restores `mcp.wolf` in an existing platform config instead of leaving it missing.
- **`differ` files visible only as a counter in `--check` (REQ-01)**: all paths are printed.
- **`protect` and the pass disagreement**: adding/removing the `protect` key is no longer treated as a local edit; mutating a protected file was never a decision before and still is not.

### Upgrade notes

- **Install and look first**: `npm i -g mister-wolf@2.20.0`, then `wolf upgrade --check` — read-only, exit `0`, per-project class plus a `Protections` section with every protected file. Nothing is written by `--check`, in the park or with `--here`.
- **State migration is automatic but one-way**: `.opencode/wolf-sync-state.json` keeps legacy string hashes readable (`origin: 'render'`, base from the stamp) and rewrites them as `{hash, origin, base}` on the next saving operation. A rollback is the expensive direction: **2.19.0 and older read the new object records as `mutated` for every file in the witness** (it compares `sha256(current)` to a recorded string). No content is lost — mutated files are never overwritten — and the heal is either a re-upgrade to 2.20.0 or restoring/removing the witness file (a full re-render then rebuilds it). **Update the whole park, not half of it**: a project left on an old CLI, run against a project already upgraded by 2.20.0, reports `mutated` noise it cannot resolve.
- **`protect` is a 2.20.0 convention**: 2.19.0 does not know the key. A protected file will look like a locally edited stamped file there (`protect: true` is a content change for the old witness, and the extra line differs from the base), so the old CLI reports `mutated` and refuses to overwrite — safe, but noisy. Files pinned with `protect: X.Y.Z` keep that intent only after the park is on 2.20.0.
- **`diverged` faces are not addressable**: they stay visible, keep exit `2`, and are resolved by the Steward through the complaint loop, not by `--resolve`. Expect exit `2` while a face is legitimately ahead of its template.
- **Backups accumulate**: `.wolf/backup/upgrade/<date>/` has no garbage collector in this release — the tree is yours to prune (`rm -rf .wolf/backup/upgrade/<old-date>`), nothing else reads it. Restore = copy the file back, then decide (`--resolve <file>=protect` to freeze, `=take` to accept the base).
- **Orphans and out-of-map files**: a stamped file whose target disappeared from the package is reported by name; it is resolved with `--resolve <path>=protect` (sanctioned variation) or deleted by hand. `take` on it is refused (`no base for a file outside the update map`) — there is nothing to take. `wolf sync` remains a deprecated alias in 2.20.0; `wolf upgrade --here` is the supported path.
- **Silent pass**: `--yes` resolves `mutated` with backup-then-base, but never resolves a `conflict` (an unstamped file in wolf's place stays your decision) and never resolves `diverged`. Exit `2` in those cases is the contract, not a failure.
- **If a future opencode starts rejecting agent frontmatter**: the regression guard (one live `opencode run` against a `protect` file, expected `200` and zero `protect` occurrences in the request body) is the tripwire. If it fires, remove the key with `wolf upgrade --resolve <file>=keep` (a one-line frontmatter edit) — the file keeps its content, only the hold signal goes away.

## [2.19.0] - 2026-10-24

### Added — Unified update: one command updates the CLI and the whole project park

- **Unified update (REQ-01)**: `wolf upgrade` now does everything in one command — the npm phase (`npm install -g mister-wolf@latest`) is followed by an update pass over every project registered in the wolf registry. Between the phases the CLI re-executes the freshly installed binary (spawn with inherited stdio/env, no shell), so the projects pass always runs the new code and both phases stream into the same terminal. The pass re-renders stamped base-set files, updates seeded faces and strictly verifies each project against the release update map; errors of one project never stop the rest (isolation, REQ-06). Interactive confirmation renders the per-project plan before the question (REQ-02); `--yes` skips it. Exit codes: `0` done / nothing to do, `1` failed (npm error, project step error), `2` done with attention — at least one project needs a decision (e.g. a locally edited stamped file; mutated files are never overwritten). In a non-TTY run without `--yes` the pass prints the plan and stops with `Cancelled: --yes is required` (exit `0`, no changes). If the re-exec spawn fails after a successful npm phase, wolf reports `CLI updated; projects not touched — run wolf upgrade again for the project pass` (exit `1`) — the npm phase is idempotent, re-running resumes at the projects pass.
- **`wolf upgrade --check` (REQ-08)**: read-only freshness summary of the CLI and the whole park — CLI installed/available, then per-project classification `current` / `behind` (stamps differ from the update map of the available release) / `needs decision` (mutated stamped files) / `unknown` (project not covered by the release map — out of coverage does **not** mean up to date). No writes at all; a missing update map degrades the summary to stamps-only (marked unreliable). Exit is always `0`.
- **`wolf upgrade --here` (REQ-14)**: the full project update step in the current directory only — no npm self-update, no re-exec, no registry. The escape hatch for unregistered projects and targeted refreshes; refuses honestly when `.wolf/memory` is missing ("run wolf init first").
- **Update map (REQ-03/04/11)**: each release ships `update-map.json` (package `files`) — per-file categories (`new`/`changed`/`deprecated`/`removed`/`unchanged`), faces to seed and AGENTS.md policy; generated by `scripts/generate-update-map.mjs` from the actual release diff and verified by a zod schema. The project pass consumes the map of the release being installed; deletions (`removed`) execute only under the confirmed plan (ADR-4). The 2.19.0 map is deliberately all-`unchanged` (30 files, 6 faces): this wave changed the CLI machinery, not the templates — the map infrastructure ships ready for future releases.
- **Unregistered-park scan (REQ-09)**: after a completed pass, a read-only scan reports how many projects with wolf memory live outside the registry (top-5 paths by recency) with the self-healing hint — never writes, never auto-registers.
- **Pass guards**: registry entries that are worktree copies, `pinned`, or missing paths are shown in the plan and skipped by the pass; duplicate realpaths are deduplicated. A lock (`UpgradeLock`) prevents two concurrent upgrade passes over the same park.
- **`wolf sync` deprecated (REQ-07)**: superseded by `wolf upgrade --here`; the alias prints a DEPRECATED notice, will be removed in 2.20.0, and remains the regenerator of `docs/dev/INDEX.md` artifact-pipeline indexes for now.
- **Terminal output standard**: a shared report renderer (sections, `●/▲/✖` markers, summary counters, graceful plain-text degradation for non-TTY and narrow widths) now drives `wolf upgrade` in all modes — and the phase-1 banner ships the block MR. WOLF wordmark.

### Upgrade notes

- Install/refresh as usual: `npm i -g mister-wolf@2.19.0`, then `wolf upgrade` (or let the next `wolf upgrade` do both phases). No data migrations; memory (`.wolf/`) is never touched by any pass.
- The first `wolf upgrade --check` on a live park is the quickest way to see where you stand (`behind`/`needs decision`/`unknown` per project).
- If you scripted `wolf sync`, switch to `wolf upgrade --here` (the alias still works through 2.19.x and keeps `docs/dev/INDEX.md` regeneration).

## [2.18.5] - 2026-10-16

### Fixed — Router conflict bypass, session-checked claim heartbeat, delivery observability

- **Router: conflict bypass closed (2.18.5, AC1)**: the wolf-router plugin previously treated `none`, `multiple` and CLI-error results of the canonical face resolver identically, silently falling back to the legacy text search where only `owner_skill` was checked — a detected face conflict could be bypassed by picking a `proposed` face and injecting its body (confirmed by independent review of 2.18.4 on a controlled plugin fixture). Delivery is now split by resolver outcome: `unique` → the face is delivered; `none` → only a restricted legacy search over `note`/`howto`/`playbook` records with type (faces are resolver-only) and status (`active`) guards in addition to the owner guard; `multiple` → the conflict is recorded, no candidate is chosen and no methodology is injected (only the universal fallback); resolver/CLI error → a diagnosable failure (`resolution=error`), never a silent fallback. `runWolf` now resolves stdout even on non-zero exit, because the CLI contract prints machine-readable JSON (`{ok:false,error:{kind}}`) with exit 1 — `promisify(execFile)` used to lose it, turning real misses into unexplained errors.
- **Claim heartbeat: session checked by default (2.18.5, AC2)**: the standard `wolf claim heartbeat <artifact>` call now verifies the session from `WOLF_SESSION` automatically (previously only the agent name was checked, so an old session of the same agent could refresh a claim handed to a new session). Name-only compatibility remains available as an explicit legacy mode (`WOLF_CLAIM_HEARTBEAT_LEGACY=1`). Transferred ownership (`release --transfer-to`, `session_id=null`) is accepted by the first heartbeat of a new session of the same owner; a second session is refused with an explicit message. Background jobs must pass `--claim-token` from the acquired claim (documented in the command description).
- **Delivery observability (2.18.5, AC3)**: every router.log line now records `source=face|legacy|default`, `resolution=unique|none|conflict|error`, `face_id`, `version`, `hash` and the session id. Legacy deliveries are no longer logged as `variant=canonical` — the journal shows exactly how a methodology was delivered.

## [2.18.4] - 2026-10-16

### Fixed — Full heartbeat identity and canonical face resolver

- **Full heartbeat identity (2.18.4)**: `writeHeartbeat` now accepts an optional full `HeartbeatIdentity` (`owner`, `sessionId`, `pid`, `claimToken`). When provided, ownership is verified strictly: `owner` must match, `sessionId` must equal the claim's session (both `null` allowed for transferred ownership), and `claimToken` must equal the current claim's token — refusing heartbeats from a previous holder's session after re-acquisition, with the owner's name in every refusal. `HeartbeatRecord` persists `session_id` and `pid` of the writer. `wolf claim heartbeat` gains optional `--session`, `--pid` and `--claim-token` flags; without them the legacy writer-only behavior is preserved.
- **Canonical face resolver (2.18.4)**: new `resolveFace` use-case (`src/app/use-cases/resolve-face.ts`) performs a strict memory query — `type=face`, `status=active`, `owner_skill === agentId` (legacy `skill:` prefix accepted) — and returns body, version and content hash of the face. Zero or multiple active faces yield a deterministic refusal (`none` / `multiple` with a sorted candidate diagnostics), never a "first match" pick. New `wolf face resolve <owner_skill>` command exposes it as a machine channel (JSON, exit 1 on miss/conflict).
- **wolf-router plugin**: playbook delivery now tries the canonical face resolver first (`wolf face resolve`); on miss/conflict it falls back to the legacy text search with the `owner_skill` guard, preserving 2.13 howto-playbook delivery. Duplicate active faces stay visible to `wolf doctor`.

## [2.18.3] - 2026-10-15

### Fixed — Per-token claim heartbeat isolation, council analytics naming, docs & demo sync

- **Per-token claim heartbeat isolation**: `ClaimRecord` now issues a unique `claim_token` on acquisition (`${epoch}.${randomTag}`) with fallback to `acquired_at` for legacy records. Heartbeats are saved as `<slug>.<claim_token>.heartbeat`, eliminating race conditions where delayed writes from a previous holder could overwrite the heartbeat of a new claim holder. `releaseClaim` and takeover clean up their per-token files alongside legacy paths.
- **Council analytics naming**: `CouncilsView` introduces `opinions_with_claims_count` to unambiguously represent opinions with non-empty claims. `audited_count` is preserved as a deprecated alias pointing to `opinions_with_claims_count` for backwards compatibility.
- **Documentation & demo scenarios sync**: `docs/guide/user-guide.md` updates the 8 core memory types to the canonical list (`rule`, `lesson`, `decision`, `thread`, `complaint`, `tool`, `note`, `face`). `docs/guide/architecture.md` synchronizes active use cases in `src/app/use-cases/`, removing obsolete draft/learn files. `README.md` and `README.ru.md` update demo scenario 8 description to reflect routing model and session wrap-up. `scripts/demo/lib.sh` ensures non-zero exit code (`exit 1`) in `summary()` upon scenario failures.

## [2.18.2] - 2026-10-15

### Fixed — Claim heartbeat lifecycle, evidence validation, docs hygiene & coordinator face

- **Claim heartbeat lifecycle binding (F-01)**: `HeartbeatRecord` now records `claim_acquired_at` tying the heartbeat to the specific ownership session. `isStale` ignores orphaned heartbeats whose `claim_acquired_at` does not match the active claim, preventing subsequent claim holders from being spuriously flagged as stale after 10 minutes. `readHeartbeat` returns `{ corrupt: true }` on damaged JSON files so that corruption triggers fail-closed staleness. `writeHeartbeat` validates that the claim exists, is active, and is owned by `writer`. `releaseClaim` and `acquireClaim` takeover now cleanly remove stale `.heartbeat` files alongside the claim JSON.
- **EvidenceRef schema validation & council metrics (F-02)**: `EvidenceRef` now enforces that at least one of `memory_id` or `path` is present and non-empty via Zod refinement. Analytics exposes `structured_claims_count` alongside `audited_count` and filters whitespace-only claim entries.
- **Documentation & demo scripts hygiene (F-04)**: Demo scenarios `scenario-6.sh`, `scenario-7.sh`, `scenario-8.sh` and `scenario-4.sh` in `scripts/demo/` no longer invoke removed `wolf run` or `wolf learn` commands, switching to canonical `wolf propose`, `wolf doctor`, `wolf validate` and `wolf session wrap-up`. `scripts/bench/` comments cleaned of `wolf run`. Stale references to "25 активных типов" in `docs/guide/architecture.md` and `docs/guide/user-guide.md` updated to "8 core-типов + расширения". Stale `wolf thread create` invocations updated to `wolf thread add`. E2E audit `tests/e2e/docs-audit.test.ts` now enforces token cleanliness across `scripts/` as well.
- **Coordinator L0 role contract (F-03)**: In `templates/base/agents/mr-wolf.md` and `.opencode/agents/mr-wolf.md`, replaced "безликий: отсутствие playbook — данные" with the coordinator face contract (procedural coordination & complaint loop protocol).

## [2.18.1] - 2026-10-15

### Fixed — Mobile scroll horizontal overflow on docs site (390px)

- **Docs site HomeModes horizontal overflow fixed**: `.wolf-mode` cards now declare `min-width: 0`, matching the `.wolf-install-step` pattern. Previously, the lack of `min-width: 0` on the CSS Grid item caused a grid blowout on mobile viewports (e.g. 390px screen width), resulting in horizontal overflow and failing mobile layout acceptance. Tested and verified on headless Chrome at 390px viewport (`scrollWidth === 390`). Resolves blocker `mem_20260901_sayt_gorizontalnyy_overflow_390px_ot_sek_e443fd`.
- **Roadmap files synchronized**: `docs/dev/roadmap/wave-2.18.md` is updated to reflect the completed 2.18.0 wave (Council opinions v2, Claim heartbeats, Base sets polish, Docs audit); `docs/dev/roadmap/wave-2.19.md` is updated to capture Wave 2.19.0 (Async learning); `docs/dev/roadmap/wave-2.20.md` tracks the candidate Wave 2.20.0 (Archival brief).

## [2.18.0] - 2026-10-14

### Added — Council opinions v2, claim heartbeats, base-set finalization, public docs audit

- **Council opinions and syntheses get a v2 schema** (REQ-01, A9): `council-opinion` and `synthesis` carry a richer structure with auditable claims, evidence, assumptions, risks, failure conditions, counterexamples and a `confidence` level (`low` | `medium` | `high`). Legacy notes read as `claims: []` and `confidence: 'medium'` so nothing old breaks. Analytics surfaces `audited_count` and `unresolved_dissent_total`. A new procedural rule — “isolation of opinion formation” — lands in `.wolf/SKILL.md` §7 and the `wolf-brainstorm` template: each council member states their view before any reveal, so the set of opinions is itself a first-class object.
- **Single-writer claims gain a heartbeat** (REQ-03, A11): a `<slug>.heartbeat` file next to the claim keeps long-running operations alive past the PID/TTL check. Default heartbeat window is 10 minutes; the absolute TTL stays at 24h; `isStale` is `PID dead OR (now - written_at > 10 min) OR (now - acquired_at > 24h)`. The writer is the owner from `WOLF_ACTOR` (debug, not auth). A new `wolf claim heartbeat <artifact>` command drives the refresh; legacy 2.17.1 claim files (no `.heartbeat`) keep the 2.17.1 rules — `readHeartbeat` returns `null` and the heartbeat branch is skipped. Atomic replace uses a unique tmp (`hrtime.bigint()` + `Math.random()` — pid-sharing between `worker_threads` on macOS is handled) plus `rename`, so parallel writers never corrupt the JSON.
- **Base sets polished for publication** (REQ-04, B0): `templates/base/agents/*.md` stay in lock-step with the shipped `.opencode/agent/*.md`; `init-project` and `scaffold-agent` enforce `face_owner_skill === agent_id`; `sync-base-set` no longer overwrites a local mutation, divergence is reported. Re-`wolf init` on a 2.17.x project preserves steward-touched faces; nothing in the base set is overwritten.
- **Public docs audited for removed commands** (REQ-02, A10): `tests/e2e/docs-audit.test.ts` greps `docs/`, `README.md` and `README.ru.md` for `wolf learn`, `wolf run`, `Sandbox Replay`, `STOP-гейт`, `sandbox-replay` and `learn --from-session` and fails CI if any non-historical file references them. Specs that describe the removed learn/run surfaces are kept readable but marked `historical: true` in the frontmatter. The same files now state “8 core memory types + extensions” instead of a stale “7 types” count.

// REQ-05 (design §4.1): единственная точка правды о ключе `protect` в
// YAML-frontmatter. Чистая логика без I/O: грамматика (parseProtect),
// нормализация (normalizeManaged), вставка/снятие ключа построчной правкой.
// Регекса штампа `wolf:rendered` здесь нет — он живёт только в stamp.ts (§4.4).

/** Разобранное значение ключа: `true` / `X.Y.Z` / null (false, пусто, вне грамматики). */
export interface ProtectValue {
  /** Держать локальный контент (в т.ч. fail-safe для недописанного/битого значения). */
  held: boolean;
  value: 'true' | string | null;
  raw: string | null;
  /** Значение вне грамматики (`yes`, `2.19`, кавычки, пусто) — держим, но предупреждаем (ADR-5). */
  unrecognized?: true;
}

export interface InsertProtectResult {
  content: string;
  changed: boolean;
  error?: 'no-yaml-frontmatter' | 'frontmatter-unparsable';
}

interface Line {
  text: string;
  eol: '\n' | '\r\n' | '';
}

const FENCE = '---';
const KEY_LINE = /^protect\s*:\s*(.*?)\s*$/;
/** Строка ключа в блоке — для нормализации (шире грамматики: снимаем и `false`). */
const KEY_ANY = /^protect\s*:/;
const FULL_VERSION = /^\d+\.\d+\.\d+$/;

function splitLines(content: string): Line[] {
  const out: Line[] = [];
  let i = 0;
  while (i < content.length) {
    const nl = content.indexOf('\n', i);
    if (nl === -1) {
      out.push({ text: content.slice(i), eol: '' });
      break;
    }
    const crlf = nl > i && content[nl - 1] === '\r';
    out.push({ text: content.slice(i, crlf ? nl - 1 : nl), eol: crlf ? '\r\n' : '\n' });
    i = nl + 1;
  }
  return out;
}

const joinLines = (lines: Line[]): string => lines.map((l) => l.text + l.eol).join('');

/** Границы frontmatter в строках: первая строка файла — `---`, закрывающая — следующая `---`. */
function frontmatter(lines: Line[]): { open: number; close: number } | null {
  if (lines.length === 0 || lines[0].text !== FENCE) return null;
  for (let i = 1; i < lines.length; i++) {
    if (lines[i].text === FENCE) return { open: 0, close: i };
  }
  return null;
}

export function parseProtect(content: string): ProtectValue {
  const lines = splitLines(content);
  const fm = frontmatter(lines);
  if (!fm) return { held: false, value: null, raw: null };
  for (let i = fm.open + 1; i < fm.close; i++) {
    const m = lines[i].text.match(KEY_LINE);
    if (!m) continue;
    const raw = m[1];
    if (raw === '' || raw === '~' || raw === 'null') return { held: true, value: null, raw, unrecognized: true };
    if (raw === 'true') return { held: true, value: 'true', raw };
    if (raw === 'false') return { held: false, value: null, raw };
    if (FULL_VERSION.test(raw)) return { held: true, value: raw, raw };
    return { held: true, value: null, raw, unrecognized: true };
  }
  return { held: false, value: null, raw: null };
}

/** Контент без строк ключа (все они, внутри frontmatter): единая база сравнения и свидетеля. */
export function normalizeManaged(content: string): string {
  const lines = splitLines(content);
  const fm = frontmatter(lines);
  if (!fm) return content;
  const kept = lines.filter((l, i) => !(i > fm.open && i < fm.close && KEY_ANY.test(l.text)));
  return joinLines(kept);
}

export function insertProtect(content: string, value: 'true' | string): InsertProtectResult {
  const lines = splitLines(content);
  const fm0 = frontmatter(lines);
  if (!fm0) return { content, changed: false, error: 'no-yaml-frontmatter' };

  const normalized = lines.filter((l, i) => !(i > fm0.open && i < fm0.close && KEY_ANY.test(l.text)));
  const fm = frontmatter(normalized);
  if (!fm) return { content, changed: false, error: 'frontmatter-unparsable' };
  const closeEol: '\n' | '\r\n' = normalized[fm.close].eol === '\r\n' ? '\r\n' : '\n';
  const result = joinLines([
    ...normalized.slice(0, fm.close),
    { text: `protect: ${value}`, eol: closeEol },
    ...normalized.slice(fm.close),
  ]);

  // Структурная проверка ДО возврата (§4.1 п.3): границы блока, распознавание значения,
  // взаимная обратимость с нормализацией. Файл пишет вызывающий — но только валидный контент.
  const check = splitLines(result);
  const fm1 = frontmatter(check);
  if (!fm1 || fm1.close !== fm.close + 1 || check[fm1.close].text !== FENCE) {
    return { content, changed: false, error: 'frontmatter-unparsable' };
  }
  const parsed = parseProtect(result);
  const expected = value === 'true' ? parsed.held && parsed.value === 'true' : parsed.held && parsed.value === value;
  if (!expected) return { content, changed: false, error: 'frontmatter-unparsable' };
  if (reinstateProtectLine(result, value) !== result) {
    return { content, changed: false, error: 'frontmatter-unparsable' };
  }
  return { content: result, changed: result !== content };
}

/** Обратимость (в): нормализованный контент + строка ключа перед закрывающим `---`. */
function reinstateProtectLine(content: string, value: 'true' | string): string {
  const lines = splitLines(normalizeManaged(content));
  const fm = frontmatter(lines);
  if (!fm) return content;
  const eol: '\n' | '\r\n' = lines[fm.close].eol === '\r\n' ? '\r\n' : '\n';
  return joinLines([...lines.slice(0, fm.close), { text: `protect: ${value}`, eol }, ...lines.slice(fm.close)]);
}

/** Снятие: удаляет все строки ключа, остальное байт-в-байт. */
export function removeProtect(content: string): string {
  return normalizeManaged(content);
}

[0m$ [0mcat src/adapters/render/sync-state.ts; echo ===DUPWARN===; grep -rn "declared twice" src/ | head -5
// src/adapters/render/sync-state.ts
// Свидетель последнего рендера: sha256 нормализованного контента, ЗАПИСАННОГО
// рендерером, + происхождение записи (design §4.2, ADR-8) и база. `origin:'keep'`
// отличает «владелец принял локальный контент» (правило 6.5 §4.3) от «волф это
// писал», иначе следующий проход молча перезаписал бы принятое.
import { createHash } from 'crypto';
import { existsSync, mkdirSync, readFileSync, renameSync, unlinkSync, writeFileSync } from 'fs';
import { join } from 'path';
import { parseStamp } from './stamp.js';

export interface SyncEntry {
  /** sha256 нормализованного контента (вычисление — обязанность вызывающего, §4.2). */
  hash: string;
  /** render — запись рендерера/эталона; keep — владелец принял локальный контент. */
  origin: 'render' | 'keep';
  /** Версия базы, на которой сделана запись (X.Y.Z). */
  base: string;
}

/** Сырой срез файла свидетеля: легаси-строки 2.19.0 и записи `SyncEntry` вперемешку. */
export type SyncStateFiles = Record<string, string | SyncEntry>;

export interface SyncState {
  /** rel-путь от baseDir → свидетель; строка — легаси-запись 2.19.0 (читается как render). */
  files: SyncStateFiles;
}

const statePath = (baseDir: string): string => join(baseDir, '.opencode', 'wolf-sync-state.json');

export function sha256(content: string): string {
  return createHash('sha256').update(content, 'utf-8').digest('hex');
}

/** Легаси-строка → свидетель: origin render, base — из штампа файла или 0.0.0. */
function normalizeEntry(baseDir: string, rel: string, raw: string | SyncEntry): SyncEntry | null {
  if (typeof raw === 'string') {
    const abs = join(baseDir, rel);
    const stamp = existsSync(abs) ? parseStamp(readFileSync(abs, 'utf-8')) : null;
    return { hash: raw, origin: 'render', base: stamp?.set ?? '0.0.0' };
  }
  if (!raw || typeof raw !== 'object' || typeof raw.hash !== 'string') return null;
  return {
    hash: raw.hash,
    origin: raw.origin === 'keep' ? 'keep' : 'render',
    base: typeof raw.base === 'string' ? raw.base : '0.0.0',
  };
}

/** Толерантная загрузка: нет файла / битый JSON → пустое состояние (bootstrap-поведение).
 *  Легаси-строки нормализуются в памяти; файл переписывается при первой сохраняемой операции. */
export function loadSyncState(baseDir: string): SyncState {
  const p = statePath(baseDir);
  if (!existsSync(p)) return { files: {} };
  try {
    const parsed = JSON.parse(readFileSync(p, 'utf-8')) as Partial<SyncState>;
    if (parsed && typeof parsed === 'object' && parsed.files && typeof parsed.files === 'object') {
      return { files: normalizeFiles(baseDir, parsed.files) };
    }
  } catch {
    // битый JSON == нет свидетеля
  }
  return { files: {} };
}

function normalizeFiles(baseDir: string, files: Record<string, string | SyncEntry>): Record<string, SyncEntry> {
  const out: Record<string, SyncEntry> = {};
  for (const [rel, raw] of Object.entries(files)) {
    const entry = normalizeEntry(baseDir, rel, raw);
    if (entry) out[rel] = entry;
  }
  return out;
}

/** Атомарная запись (NFR-04): tmp в том же каталоге + rename; после успеха `*.tmp` нет. */
export function saveSyncState(baseDir: string, state: SyncState): void {
  const dir = join(baseDir, '.opencode');
  mkdirSync(dir, { recursive: true });
  const path = statePath(baseDir);
  const tmp = join(dir, `.wolf-sync-state.json.${process.pid}.${Date.now()}.tmp`);
  try {
    writeFileSync(tmp, `${JSON.stringify(state, null, 2)}\n`, 'utf-8');
    renameSync(tmp, path);
  } catch (err) {
    try {
      if (existsSync(tmp)) unlinkSync(tmp);
    } catch {
      // best-effort: исходная ошибка важнее
    }
    throw err;
  }
}

`WOLF_SESSION` — the env var that ties telemetry to a session — is produced by the CLI itself: every invocation without an explicit `WOLF_SESSION` gets a fresh `cli-<uuid>`, stable for the whole process (all telemetry writers of one command share it). An explicitly exported `WOLF_SESSION` is never overwritten. The stamped opencode plugins (`wolf-router`, `wolf-session-start`) pass a fresh `opc-<uuid>` on every CLI spawn — the inherited env of a long-lived opencode process would otherwise collapse all deliveries into one fake session. Plugin templates are updated by the projects pass of `wolf upgrade` (`--here` for one project).

`wolf mcp` is deliberately not sessioned: the MCP server is a long-lived process with a single environment, so one session id for all requests would be false attribution — its telemetry is written with `session_id: null` (see [Telemetry](/guide/telemetry)).

## wolf upgrade

**Unified update (2.19.0): one command updates everything.** The npm phase (`npm install -g mister-wolf@latest`) is followed by an update pass over every project registered in the wolf registry: stamped base-set files are re-rendered, seeded faces updated, and the update map is strictly verified (see [Base Set](/guide/base-set)). The CLI re-executes itself between the phases, so the projects pass always runs with the freshly installed binary; the output of both phases flows into the same terminal.

**Owner intentions (2.20.0)** add the missing half: named decisions, four resolution verbs, an in-file hold (`protect`) and a machine channel. The full workflow — with real output and every limitation — is on [Upgrading a Project and a Park](/guide/upgrade); this section is the flag reference.

```text
Usage: wolf upgrade [options]
```

Options:

- `--check` — read-only freshness summary of the CLI and the whole park: no install, no writes, exit always `0`; also prints the `Protections` section (default: false)
- `--here` — update only the current project: the full project step in cwd, no npm self-update, no registry
- `--yes` — apply the plan without interactive confirmation (`--here` or the projects pass); `mutated` files are backed up and taken, `conflict`/`diverged` still keep exit `2`
- `--resolve <target=verb>` — resolve a decision: `<file|N>=take|keep|protect|pin`, repeatable, comma-separated lists allowed
- `--show <file|N>` — print one target with the `local → base` diff (read-only)
- `--review` — walk the decisions one by one with a diff (TTY); without a TTY prints the recipe and writes nothing
- `--json` — print a single machine-readable document on stdout (`wolf.upgrade/1`, or `wolf.upgrade.check/1` with `--check`)

```bash
wolf upgrade                              # npm phase + projects pass (plan, then confirm)
wolf upgrade --check                      # read-only: CLI + park freshness + protections
wolf upgrade --here                       # update this project only, outside the registry
wolf upgrade --review                     # decide per file, with a diff
wolf upgrade --resolve 2=take --resolve .opencode/agents/foo.md=protect
wolf upgrade --show 2                     # what would `take` do to this file?
wolf upgrade --yes --json > run.json      # silent pass, machine channel
```

Flag dispatch happens once, in the command action, and short-circuits before the npm phase and the re-exec: `--check` (stronger than `--here`), then `--show`, `--review`, `--resolve`, then `--here`, and only a bare `wolf upgrade` / `--yes` reaches the npm phase. Incompatible pairs are argument errors (exit `1`, nothing written): `--review` with `--json`/`--resolve`/`--yes`, `--show` with `--resolve`/`--yes`, `--resolve` with `--yes`, `--check` with `--resolve`.

Actual `--check` output (2.20.0, a two-project fixture park, one protected file each — absolute paths shortened):

```text

## Storage layout

What `wolf init` creates and what appears lazily:

```text
.wolf/
├── config.yaml            # project config
├── memory/
│   ├── threads/<tid>/     # created by init; object layout v2:
│   │   └── <subdir>/<id>.md   # threads/<tid>/<subdir>/<id>.md
│   ├── shared/<subdir>/   # created by init; shared/<subdir>/<id>.md
│   ├── briefs/            # created by init
│   ├── events.jsonl       # lazy
│   ├── relations.jsonl    # lazy
│   └── quarantine/        # lazy; broken objects quarantined by wolf validate --fix
├── cache/
│   ├── index.sqlite       # lazy; SQLite FTS search index
│   └── sessions/          # lazy; per-session delivery registries (dedup), GC after 7 days
├── metrics/               # lazy; session-metrics.jsonl, patterns.jsonl, signal-counts.json (derived counters)
├── thinking/              # lazy; structured thinking sequences
├── tools/                 # lazy; registered tool scripts
└── backup/<ts>/           # lazy; config backups from wolf init --recreate
```

A work thread lives at `memory/threads/<tid>/WORK-THREAD.md`.

## Platform configs and `mcp.wolf`

Platform configs (`opencode.json`, Claude's `.mcp.json`) are written by `wolf init --platform <ids>` — the list replaces the current set — and never by a plain minimal `init`. What a minimal `init` does with them since 2.20.0 is **repair only**:

- platform config file **exists** and the platform is detected → a missing or corrupted `mcp.wolf` entry is restored (alongside `default_agent` / `subagent_depth`), the rest of the JSON is preserved untouched;
- the entry is already canonical → `unchanged` (idempotent; a second `init` writes nothing);
- the config file **does not exist** → nothing is created — a minimal init stays minimal, and `--platform opencode` remains the explicit way to write a platform config;
- the config is broken JSONC → not touched at all: `init` reports `skipped` with the reason and exits `0` (`wolf init --recreate` is the escape hatch for a corrupted `.wolf/config.yaml`, not for platform configs).

This is what makes the `wolf doctor` advice (`run wolf init` when the wolf entry is missing) actionable: after a successful repair the doctor no longer reports `wolf entry missing`. Foreign MCP entries in the same file are never touched.

## Global config

User-level (cross-project) configuration lives under `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME/wolf`, or `~/.config/wolf` when `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` is not set.

## Schema version

The current store schema version is **2**; legacy projects without the `schema_version` marker are version 1. `wolf doctor` checks all registered projects for binary vs schema version mismatches, verifies platform configs and prunes dead entries:

```bash
wolf doctor
```

[0m$ [0msed -n '95,165p' docs/site/guide/troubleshooting.md
- **Symptom:** `wolf add` with the same title twice (within the id millisecond window) exits non-zero instead of writing the object:
  `Memory object "mem_…" already exists (same title produced same id).` followed by `Use: wolf edit <id> (правка) | wolf supersede <id> <new-id> (смена) | другой --title.`
- **Cause:** before 2.14 a same-id `add` silently **overwrote** the existing object — data loss dressed as success. The guard turns that into an explicit refusal.
- **Fix:** pick the lane the hint offers — `wolf edit <id>` to change the existing object, `wolf supersede <old> <new>` to replace it with a successor, or a different `--title` for a genuinely new object.
- **Audit trail:** any save that does overwrite a file (there are legitimate paths) records a `memory.overwritten` event with the previous title — silent overwrites no longer exist; the event log is the proof.

## 10. `upgrade`: CLI updated, projects were not touched

- **Symptom:** `wolf upgrade` updated the CLI (npm phase succeeded) but ended with `CLI updated; projects not touched — run wolf upgrade again for the project pass` and exit code 1.
- **Cause:** the projects pass re-executes the freshly installed `wolf` binary; if that spawn fails (for example the binary moved between npm and the re-exec), wolf refuses to guess and stops without touching any project.
- **Fix:** simply run `wolf upgrade` again — the npm phase is idempotent (up-to-date skips straight to the projects pass) and the pass resumes with the new binary. Check `wolf upgrade --check` afterwards to confirm the park freshness.

## 11. `mutated` on a file you want to keep, release after release

- **Symptom:** a stamped agent/skill/command was edited locally; every `wolf upgrade --check` reports the project as `needs decision`, and you do not want the base version.
- **Cause:** the file is stamped, the witness recorded the render, and the local content differs from it — wolf refuses to choose for you (and never overwrites).
- **Fix:** declare the intent in one command instead of living with the noise:

  ```bash
  wolf upgrade --resolve <file>=protect   # hold forever: protect: true in the frontmatter
  wolf upgrade --resolve <file>=pin       # hold and record the current base: protect: X.Y.Z
  ```

  `keep` is the soft version: it accepts the current content once (`origin: keep`) without protecting it — the next local edit makes it a decision again. A protected file still shows in the `Protections` section of `--check`, but no longer as `needs decision`. Audit the whole machine with `grep -rn '^protect:' ~/dev/*/.opencode/agents`.

## 12. Broken onboarding markers in `AGENTS.md`

- **Symptom:** `AGENTS.md` is no longer updated at all; `wolf upgrade --check` shows the project as `unknown` with the detail `onboarding markers broken`, and the pass reports the file as `foreign`/skipped.
- **Cause:** the block is delimited by `<!-- wolf:onboarding v3 -->` and `<!-- /wolf:onboarding -->`; when the closing line is missing (or the opening one is gone), wolf cannot tell its zone from yours and applies the `wx` policy — hands off.
- **Fix:** restore the closing marker line by hand (at the end of the wolf block), then run `wolf upgrade --here --check` — the file is managed again and the block is updated in place. If you would rather own the file, leave the markers broken: wolf will not touch it, and the report will keep saying so — that state is honest, not an error.

## 13. A hand-edited frontmatter that no longer parses

- **Symptom:** after editing an agent/skill frontmatter by hand, the agent silently disappears from opencode, or `wolf upgrade --resolve <file>=protect` answers `cannot protect <path>: no YAML frontmatter — add one or use keep` / refuses with `frontmatter-unparsable`.
- **Cause:** wolf validates the frontmatter block structurally before writing the `protect` key — the block boundaries, the inserted value and its reversibility. It does not repair YAML it did not write: a file could already have been broken before wolf touched it. (A broken agent frontmatter makes opencode drop the agent without a word — that is the reason for the check, not pedantry.)
- **Fix:** compare with the base and take it, or fix the block by hand:

  ```bash
  wolf upgrade --show <file>            # local vs base, read-only
  wolf upgrade --resolve <file>=take    # base version, local copy saved to .wolf/backup/upgrade/<date>/
  ```

  If the file has no frontmatter at all and you want to keep your version, `keep` is the legal verb — `protect` deliberately refuses to invent a block.

## 14. Restoring a file from the upgrade backup

- **Symptom:** an overwrite took local content you still need (`--yes`, `take`, or a removal).
- **Cause:** that is what the backup is for — every overwrite and every deletion copies the file to `.wolf/backup/upgrade/<YYYY-MM-DD>/<rel-path>` first (the same file overwritten twice in one day becomes `<name>-2.<ext>`, then `-3`, … — `SKILL.md` → `SKILL-2.md`).
- **Fix:** copy it back and decide:

  ```bash
  find .wolf/backup/upgrade -path '*<file-fragment>*'
  cp .wolf/backup/upgrade/2026-10-25/.opencode/skills/foo/SKILL.md .opencode/skills/foo/SKILL.md
  wolf upgrade --here --resolve .opencode/skills/foo/SKILL.md=protect   # freeze it, so it stops being a decision
  ```

  The digest of the run names the copy for every overwrite (`local saved: …`), and `--json` lists them in `projects[].backups[]`. There is no garbage collector: prune old dates yourself. Permissions and timestamps are not preserved.

YAML-frontmatter, а ручной frontmatter не делает AGENTS.md защищаемым — глаголы
`protect`/`pin` отвечают отказом «нет frontmatter» (матрица §4.8). Намерение
выражается выбором режима: блочный сохраняет текст вне блока, штампованный
принадлежит wolf целиком (ADR-7).

1. **Whole-file (штампованный) AGENTS.md** — поведение 2.19.0 без изменения:
   сравнение всего файла, свидетель, `mutated` при локальной правке, полная
   перезапись при `differs`; правила 6/6.5/7 §4.3 применяются как к любому
   штампованному файлу (`keep` на AGENTS.md держится по 6.5). Тихий режим
   `--yes` берёт mutated AGENTS.md **тем же** механизмом, что файлы карты:
   `syncBaseSet(..., { takeMutated: { backup } })` → правило 6′ «бэкап → эталон →
   свидетель» (§4.12); `opts` пробрасывается в `syncAgentsMd`, второго пути
   бэкапа нет. Правило 7 для AGENTS.md тоже требует бэкап, если записи свидетеля
   нет (NFR-05).
2. **Блочный (append) AGENTS.md** — файл без штампа, но с маркером. Управляется
   **только блок между маркерами**; текст выше и ниже — байт-в-байт, блок
   наследует EOL закрывающего маркера (CRLF-файл не смешивает `\r\n`/`\n`,
   m-7; допущение: однородный файл — норма, смешанный наследует EOL маркера).

**Init на штампованном файле с блоком (миграция 2.19.0 → 2.20.0).** Проект,
инициализированный 2.19.0, имеет `AGENTS.md` со штампом `set=2.19.0` и блоком
`v2`. `wolf init` обновляет блок по версии формата и **приводит штамп к текущей
базе на его месте** (позицию задаёт `insertStamp`: после YAML-frontmatter, `---`
остаётся первой строкой); свидетель пишется по нормализованному контенту, но только если файл
совпал с эталоном (EOL отличием не считается). Иначе (пользовательский текст вне
блока) свидетель не переписывается и файл остаётся `mutated` — иначе следующий

/** Проект, до которого проход не дошёл: только статус плана (design §4.13). */
function plannedProject(plan: ProjectPlan): JsonProject {
  return {
    name: plan.name,
    path: plan.path,
    status: 'skipped',
    ...(plan.status !== null ? { skipReason: plan.status } : {}),
    files: [],
    protected: [],
    backups: [],
  };
}

/** Файлы прогона для `--json.projects[].files`: путь, код, признак карты. */
export function jsonProjectFiles(report: ProjectReport, map: UpdateMap): JsonProject['files'] {
  const inMap = new Set<string>(['AGENTS.md', ...map.files.map((e) => projectPath(e))]);
  return report.sync.outcomes.map((o) => {
    const code = o.protected
      ? 'protected'
      : o.kept
        ? 'kept'
        : o.action === 'skipped'
          ? o.mutated
            ? 'skipped-mutated'
            : 'skipped-identical'
          : o.action === 'appended'
            ? 'updated'
            : o.action;
    return { path: o.file, code, inMap: inMap.has(o.file) };
  });
}

export async function runUpgradeProjects(
  deps: UpgradeProjectsDeps,
  opts: UpgradeProjectsOpts = {}
): Promise<UpgradeRunResult> {
  const lock = deps.upgradeLock;
  if (lock) {
    try {
      lock.acquire(LOCK_ID);
    } catch (err) {
      // чужой живой lock: не снимаем (release удалил бы файл владельца)
      return guardFailure(msg(err));
    }
  }
  try {
    return await runPass(deps, opts);
  } finally {
    lock?.release(LOCK_ID);
  }
}

  let registered: RegisteredProject[];

export function verifyNameLines(verify: VerifyResult, map: UpdateMap): ReportNode[] {
  const out: ReportNode[] = [];
  const kinds = kindIndex(map);
  const named = verify.items.filter((i) => i.needsDecision && i.code !== 'diverged');
  if (named.length > 0) {
    out.push(
      line('warn', `needs decision: ${named.map((i) => `${displayName(i, kinds)} (${i.code})`).join(', ')}`, true)
    );
  }
  const protectedCount = verify.items.filter((i) => i.code === 'protected').length;
  if (protectedCount > 0) out.push(line('info', `${protectedCount} protected (intentional)`, true));
  const kept = verify.items.filter((i) => i.code === 'kept').map((i) => displayName(i, kinds));
  if (kept.length > 0) out.push(line('info', `${kept.length} kept (local): ${kept.join(', ')}`, true));
  return out;
}

/** Дайджест бэкапов проекта (§4.12): файл и путь копии; take — «took base»,
 *  удаление — «removed». Единственная реализация формата: проход и `--here`. */
export function backupDigestLines(report: ProjectReport): ReportNode[] {
  const out: ReportNode[] = [];
  for (const b of report.backups ?? []) {
    const took = report.sync.outcomes.some((o) => o.backup === b.backup);
    out.push(line('info', `${took ? 'took base for' : 'removed'} ${b.file} (local saved: ${b.backup})`, true));
  }
  return out;
}

// src/adapters/render/sync-state.ts
// Свидетель последнего рендера: sha256 нормализованного контента, ЗАПИСАННОГО
// рендерером, + происхождение записи (design §4.2, ADR-8) и база. `origin:'keep'`
// отличает «владелец принял локальный контент» (правило 6.5 §4.3) от «волф это
// писал», иначе следующий проход молча перезаписал бы принятое.
import { createHash } from 'crypto';
import { existsSync, mkdirSync, readFileSync, renameSync, unlinkSync, writeFileSync } from 'fs';
import { join } from 'path';
import { parseStamp } from './stamp.js';

export interface SyncEntry {
  /** sha256 нормализованного контента (вычисление — обязанность вызывающего, §4.2). */
  hash: string;
  /** render — запись рендерера/эталона; keep — владелец принял локальный контент. */
  origin: 'render' | 'keep';
  /** Версия базы, на которой сделана запись (X.Y.Z). */
  base: string;
}

export interface SyncState {
  /** rel-путь от baseDir → свидетель; строка — легаси-запись 2.19.0 (читается как render). */
  files: Record<string, string | SyncEntry>;
}

const statePath = (baseDir: string): string => join(baseDir, '.opencode', 'wolf-sync-state.json');

export function sha256(content: string): string {
  return createHash('sha256').update(content, 'utf-8').digest('hex');
}

[0m$ [0mgit show 000c289:src/adapters/render/sync-state.ts | sed -n '20,55p'
/** Толерантная загрузка: нет файла / битый JSON → пустое состояние (bootstrap-поведение). */
export function loadSyncState(baseDir: string): SyncState {
  const p = statePath(baseDir);
  if (!existsSync(p)) return { files: {} };
  try {
    const parsed = JSON.parse(readFileSync(p, 'utf-8')) as Partial<SyncState>;
    if (parsed && typeof parsed === 'object' && parsed.files && typeof parsed.files === 'object') {
      return { files: parsed.files as Record<string, string> };
    }
  } catch {
    // битый JSON == нет свидетеля
  }
  return { files: {} };
}

export function saveSyncState(baseDir: string, state: SyncState): void {
  mkdirSync(join(baseDir, '.opencode'), { recursive: true });
  writeFileSync(statePath(baseDir), `${JSON.stringify(state, null, 2)}\n`, 'utf-8');
}

// src/adapters/render/templates-root.ts
import { readFileSync, existsSync } from 'fs';
import { dirname, join } from 'path';
import { fileURLToPath } from 'url';

let cachedRoot: string | null = null;

export function packageRoot(): string {
  if (cachedRoot) return cachedRoot;
  let dir = dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url));
  for (let i = 0; i < 8; i++) {
    if (existsSync(join(dir, 'package.json'))) {
      cachedRoot = dir;
      return dir;
    }
    dir = dirname(dir);
  }
  throw new Error(`package.json not found upward from ${fileURLToPath(import.meta.url)}`);
}

export function templatesRoot(): string {
  return join(packageRoot(), 'templates', 'base');
}

/** Harness-специфичные шаблоны (плагины) — вне нейтрального base (M6, спека §7.3). */
export function harnessTemplatesRoot(harnessId: string): string {
  return join(packageRoot(), 'templates', harnessId);
}

export function wolfVersion(): string {
  const pkg = JSON.parse(readFileSync(join(packageRoot(), 'package.json'), 'utf-8')) as { version: string };
  return pkg.version;
}

async list(filters?: ListFilters): Promise<MemoryObject[]> {
    const seen = new Map<string, { obj: MemoryObject; isLegacy: boolean }>();
    let roots = this.roots();
    if (filters?.type) {
      const typeRoots = await this.typeScanRoots(filters.type);
      if (typeRoots !== null) roots = typeRoots;
    }
    for (let ri = 0; ri < roots.length; ri++) {
      const root = roots[ri];
      const isLegacy = ri === roots.length - 1;
      const files = await this.walkMarkdownFiles(root);
      for (const path of files) {
        const parsed = await this.parseFileSafe(path);
        if (!parsed) continue;
        if (filters?.type && parsed.type !== filters.type) continue;
        const existing = seen.get(parsed.id);
        if (!existing) {
          seen.set(parsed.id, { obj: parsed, isLegacy });
        } else if (!isLegacy && existing.isLegacy) {
          this.onProblem?.(`Duplicate id ${parsed.id}: new layout overrides legacy`);
          seen.set(parsed.id, { obj: parsed, isLegacy });
        }
      }
    }
    let results = Array.from(seen.values(), (v) => v.obj);
    if (filters?.status) results = results.filter((o) => o.status === filters.status);
    if (filters?.stale) results = results.filter((o) => isStale(o));
    // P212 (2.13 §5.3б): фасет — postfilter по frontmatter (alias-резолв уже
    // инжектировал facet старым типам); значение не из словаря → пусто
    if (filters?.facet) results = results.filter((o) => o.facet === filters.facet);
    return results;
  }

  async update(id: string, patch: Partial<MemoryObject>): Promise<MemoryObject> {
    const located = await this.locate(id);
    if (!located) throw new Error(`Memory object not found: ${id}`);
    const updated: MemoryObject = { ...located.obj, ...patch, updated_at: new Date().toISOString() };
    // P211: alias-объект при первом update переезжает на канонический путь/тип;
    // transient alias_origin умирает — повторный update идемпотентен
    delete (updated as { alias_origin?: string }).alias_origin;
    await this.save(updated);
    const newPath = targetPathFor(this.baseDir, updated, this.projectDeclarations());
    if (located.path !== newPath) {
      try {
        await fs.unlink(located.path);
      } catch (err) {
        if (!isEnoent(err)) throw err;
      }
    }
    return updated;
  }

  async scanProblems(): Promise<{ path: string; error: string }[]> {
    const problems: { path: string; error: string }[] = [];
    for (const root of this.roots()) {
      let files: string[];
      try {
        files = await this.walkMarkdownFiles(root);
      } catch (err) {

216:`AGENTS.md` защитить нельзя: в штатном шаблоне у него нет frontmatter, и ручной frontmatter защищаемым его не делает. Используйте два режима владения — блочный сохраняет ваш текст вне маркеров, штампованный whole-file означает, что файлом владеет волф целиком.
279:- **Блочный (по умолчанию для проекта, инициализированного волфом).** В файле есть `<!-- wolf:onboarding v3 -->` … `<!-- /wolf:onboarding -->`. Волф владеет только строками между маркерами; всё выше и ниже — байт-в-байт ваше (CRLF-файл сохраняет CRLF). Пользовательский текст **внутри** блока не сохраняется — блок зона волфа.

## [2.20.0] - 2026-10-25

### Added — Upgrade UX: owner intentions, named decisions, silent pass

- **File names in the pass and in `--check` (REQ-01)**: every needs-decision outcome (`mutated` / `diverged` / `conflict`) and every `differ` is now printed with the file path relative to the project root, not just as a counter — e.g. `mutated: .opencode/agents/worker-reviewer.md · 1 protected (intentional)`. Counters are preserved; names are added next to them. Long lists are wrapped by the terminal renderer, not truncated.
- **`wolf upgrade --resolve` (REQ-06)**: owner decisions without a pass — `--resolve <file|N>=take|keep|protect|pin`, repeatable, comma-separated lists allowed. Targets: `<rel-path>` (unique in the park or in `--here`), `<project>/<rel-path>`, or the decision number `N` from `--review`/`--check` (a path that starts with a digit is written as `./3=…`). All parse and scope errors are collected and printed before anything is written; decisions are applied in registry order, each one idempotent (`backup → file → witness`). A protected file, an out-of-map `orphan`, and a non-mutated target are all addressable by path; `take` is the only verb stronger than `protect` (it drops the key, takes the base and saves the local content to a backup). `keep` on a `conflict` is refused with a hint (`use protect (hold) or take (base)`), `protect` on a file without YAML frontmatter is refused (`cannot protect <path>: no YAML frontmatter — add one or use keep`). `--resolve` never runs the npm phase or the pass.
- **`wolf upgrade --review` (REQ-07)**: walks the decisions one by one with a real diff (`t` take / `k` keep / `p` protect / `v` pin / `s` skip, plus `a` — apply to all similar targets of the same kind+code, `q` — quit; accepted decisions stay applied). Without a TTY it writes nothing and prints the numbered recipe plus the exact commands, exit `2`. The same review engine drives `--resolve`.
---
---
title: История версий
---

История версий синхронизируется автоматически из [CHANGELOG.md](https://github.com/chekh/mister-wolf/blob/main/CHANGELOG.md) при каждой сборке сайта. Описания изменений приведены на английском — в том же виде, что в npm и GitHub Releases.

# Changelog

All notable changes to this project are documented in this file.

Format: [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.1.0/); versioning: [SemVer](https://semver.org/).

## [2.20.0] - 2026-10-25

### Added — Upgrade UX: owner intentions, named decisions, silent pass

- **File names in the pass and in `--check` (REQ-01)**: every needs-decision outcome (`mutated` / `diverged` / `conflict`) and every `differ` is now printed with the file path relative to the project root, not just as a counter — e.g. `mutated: .opencode/agents/worker-reviewer.md · 1 protected (intentional)`. Counters are preserved; names are added next to them. Long lists are wrapped by the terminal renderer, not truncated.
- **`wolf upgrade --resolve` (REQ-06)**: owner decisions without a pass — `--resolve <file|N>=take|keep|protect|pin`, repeatable, comma-separated lists allowed. Targets: `<rel-path>` (unique in the park or in `--here`), `<project>/<rel-path>`, or the decision number `N` from `--review`/`--check` (a path that starts with a digit is written as `./3=…`). All parse and scope errors are collected and printed before anything is written; decisions are applied in registry order, each one idempotent (`backup → file → witness`). A protected file, an out-of-map `orphan`, and a non-mutated target are all addressable by path; `take` is the only verb stronger than `protect` (it drops the key, takes the base and saves the local content to a backup). `keep` on a `conflict` is refused with a hint (`use protect (hold) or take (base)`), `protect` on a file without YAML frontmatter is refused (`cannot protect <path>: no YAML frontmatter — add one or use keep`). `--resolve` never runs the npm phase or the pass.
- **`wolf upgrade --review` (REQ-07)**: walks the decisions one by one with a real diff (`t` take / `k` keep / `p` protect / `v` pin / `s` skip, plus `a` — apply to all similar targets of the same kind+code, `q` — quit; accepted decisions stay applied). Without a TTY it writes nothing and prints the numbered recipe plus the exact commands, exit `2`. The same review engine drives `--resolve`.
- **`wolf upgrade --show` (REQ-08)**: read-only view of one target — current `protect` value, base, file stamp, witness, drift and the `local → base` diff (what `take` would give you), plus the ready-to-run `release:` command. Works for protected files and for files outside the update map (there: `base: n/a (file outside the update map)`, no diff, no `take` hint).

- **Path A — memory:** `wolf init` connects the project minimally — the `.wolf/` skeleton and `AGENTS.md`; it writes no platform configs and no base set. What a clean run prints: `memory skeleton: ensured`, `base set: AGENTS.md created`, the init-report id.
- **Path B — OpenCode organization:** `wolf init --full --platform opencode` renders the full base set (6 agent frames, 19 skills, 3 commands, 2 plugins, seeded playbook faces) and writes `opencode.json` (`mcp.wolf`, `default_agent=mr-wolf`, `subagent_depth=2`). **Restart opencode** after it — the MCP server and the default agent connect at startup. Claude Code will ask you to approve the project-scope MCP server on first start — that is expected.

- **Try it without installing:** `npx mister-wolf init` — try-out mode: creates the `.wolf/` memory skeleton but never writes MCP configs or the base set. Like it? `npm install -g mister-wolf` and run `wolf init` again.
- **Updating:** `wolf upgrade` — one command updates everything: the npm phase (`npm install -g mister-wolf@latest`) is followed by the update pass over all registered projects (stamped base-set files and seeded faces; deletions only with confirmation). `--check` reports CLI + park freshness without installing; `--here` updates only the current project (no npm, no registry); `--yes` skips the confirmation and backs up whatever it overwrites. Dev/linked installs are refused honestly. `wolf sync` is deprecated → `wolf upgrade --here` (still available in 2.20.0).
- **Owner intent (2.20.0):** when a pass needs your word, it names the file and the reason (`mutated` / `conflict`, per path — no bare counters). `--review` walks the decisions with a diff, `--show <file|N>` prints local vs base, `--resolve <file|N>=take|keep|protect|pin` applies one, `--json` is the machine channel (`wolf.upgrade/1`). `protect: true` (or `protect: X.Y.Z`) in an agent/skill/command frontmatter holds that file forever — never overwritten, never deleted, still visible in `--check`. A local conflict, start to finish:

  ```bash
  wolf upgrade --check                                     # needs decision: .opencode/agents/foo.md (mutated)
  wolf upgrade --show 1                                    # local → base diff, read-only
  wolf upgrade --resolve 1=protect                         # this file is mine from now on
  wolf upgrade --check                                     # current · 1 protected (intentional)
  ```

  Full workflow, backups and limits: [Upgrading a Project and a Park](docs/site/guide/upgrade.md).

- **Platforms v1:** opencode, Claude Code. Platform MCP configs are opt-in: `wolf init --platform opencode,claude` (the list replaces the current set); without the flag no platform configs are written.
- **First run without barriers:** `wolf init` decides nothing for you — a minimal file set by default (`--full` for the complete base set), one interview question in a TTY (Enter to skip), no model pinning (`--model` is opt-in; model lines are omitted and the platform default applies). Re-init on a live project shows the plan and needs a confirmation (`--yes` skips it).
---RU---
- **Путь А — память:** `wolf init` подключает проект минимально — скелет `.wolf/` и `AGENTS.md`; платформенные конфиги и базовый сет он не пишет. Что печатает чистый запуск: `memory skeleton: ensured`, `base set: AGENTS.md created`, id init-отчёта.
- **Путь Б — организация OpenCode:** `wolf init --full --platform opencode` отрисовывает полный базовый набор (6 рамок агентов, 19 скиллов, 3 команды, 2 плагина, посев playbook-лиц) и пишет `opencode.json` (`mcp.wolf`, `default_agent=mr-wolf`, `subagent_depth=2`). После него **перезапусти opencode** — MCP-сервер и агент по умолчанию подключаются при старте. Claude Code при первом старте попросит approve project-scope MCP-сервер — это штатно.

- **Попробовать без установки:** `npx mister-wolf init` — try-out-режим: создаёт скелет памяти `.wolf/`, но никогда не пишет ни MCP-конфиги, ни базовый сет. Понравилось — `npm install -g mister-wolf` и повтори `wolf init`.
- **Обновление:** `wolf upgrade` — одна команда обновляет всё: npm-фаза
  (`npm install -g mister-wolf@latest`) и следом проход по всем зарегистрированным проектам
  (штампованные файлы базового сета и посеянные лица; удаления — только по подтверждению).
  `--check` — read-only сводка свежести CLI и парка; `--here` — обновить только текущий проект
  (без npm и реестра); `--yes` — без интерактивного подтверждения, с бэкапом всего, что
  перезаписывает. Dev/linked-установке откажет честно. `wolf sync` депрекирован →
  `wolf upgrade --here` (алиас ещё работает в 2.20.0).
- **Намерения владельца (2.20.0):** когда проходу нужно ваше слово, он называет файл и причину
  (`mutated` / `conflict`, по путям — без голых счётчиков). `--review` разбирает решения с диффом,
  `--show <файл|N>` печатает локальный vs эталон, `--resolve <файл|N>=take|keep|protect|pin`
  применяет решение, `--json` — машинный канал (`wolf.upgrade/1`). `protect: true` (или
  `protect: X.Y.Z`) во frontmatter agent/skill/command-файла держит файл навсегда — не
  перезаписывается, не удаляется и остаётся видимым в `--check`. Локальный конфликт целиком:
